We treat these as exchangeable because SDF at $\\lambda = 0.005$ produces no separation from LoRA-only. The $\\lambda = 0.01$ mean of 56.33\\% falls within $0.8\\sigma$ of this null ($p > 0.1$), consistent with seed-to-seed variation rather than a treatment effect. The $\\lambda = 0.1$ 30K mean of 58.25\\% (2 seeds) yields $z = 1.85$ ($p \\approx 0.032$), a marginal departure whose interpretation is limited by $n = 2$.\n\nA Phase~2 ablation reinforces this conclusion. Setting $\\lambda = 0$ during Phase~2 (steps 5K--30K), so that SDF supervision is active only during auxiliary head warmup (Phase~1, steps 0--5K), yields CAR of 57.5\\% (single seed, s2). This value lies within the null range, indicating that SDF training produces no lasting behavioral effect on collision avoidance.\n\n\\section{Step-wise Checkpoint Analysis for \\texorpdfstring{$\\lambda = 0.1$}{lambda = 0.1}}\n\\label{sec:appendix_stepwise}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:stepwise_lambda01} reports per-seed CAR at seven checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$. Seed-1 data is available at 16K, 20K, 22K, and 30K; 24K--28K report seed-0 only. The seed-0 curve shows no monotonic trend through 28K; the 30K checkpoint marks the first consistent dual-seed departure from the null band, though whether this reflects a genuine late-training effect or sampling variance at $n = 2$ remains open.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed CAR (\\%) at training checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$ (200 episodes each). 24K--28K: seed-0 only. Dual-seed means remain within the null band (54.67\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,1.94\\%) at all checkpoints except 30K, where both seeds depart marginally ($z = 1.85$, $p \\approx 0.032$).}\n\\label{tab:stepwise_lambda01}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\nCheckpoint & Seed 0 & Seed 1 & Mean \\\\\n\\midrule\n16K steps & 55.5 & 56.0 & 55.75 \\\\\n20K steps & 55.0 & 56.0 & 55.5 \\\\\n22K steps & 60.5 & 56.5 & 58.5 \\\\\n24K steps & 52.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n26K steps & 53.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n28K steps & 54.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n30K steps & 58.0 & 58.5 & 58.25 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nIndividual seeds exhibit checkpoint-to-checkpoint CAR fluctuations of up to 8\\,pp (seed~0: 52.5--60.5\\%), larger than the cross-seed standard deviation at any fixed checkpoint ($\\leq$2.8\\,pp). This is consistent with binomial sampling noise at $n = 50$ episodes per task ($\\sigma \\approx 7$\\,pp per task for $p \\approx 0.5$). The seed-averaged curve shows no monotonic trend, confirming that SDF loss weight does not produce a progressive training signal.\n\n\n\\section{Oracle SDF Refinement Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_oracle}\n\nAll oracle variants replace the learned SDF decoder with ground-truth distance fields computed from the simulation geometry. Each is evaluated for 160 episodes (4 tasks $\\times$ 40).\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-adaptive.} At each timestep, if the ground-truth SDF at the end-effector position falls below a safety margin, the action is adjusted by a step in the negative SDF gradient direction. The step size $\\alpha$ is scaled inversely with distance: smaller distances produce larger corrections. This mimics an adaptive potential field controller.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-fixed.} Identical to Oracle-adaptive except the step size is fixed at $\\alpha = 0.005$ regardless of distance. This isolates the effect of step-size adaptation from the refinement geometry.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-feature.} Instead of modifying the action directly in Cartesian space, this variant applies the SDF gradient in the policy's latent feature space via backpropagation. The gradient of the ground-truth SDF value with respect to the intermediate features is computed and used to perturb the features before the action head decodes them. This tests whether geometric corrections are more effective when applied in the model's learned representation space. In practice, the feature-space variant fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements per episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49 in action space).\n\n\n\\section{Training Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_training}\n\n\\paragraph{Architecture.}\nWe use LoRA~\\citep{hu2022lora} adapters injected into the PaliGemma vision encoder (rank 16, $\\alpha$=16) and the action expert (rank 32, $\\alpha$=32), following the configuration of OpenPi. The SDF decoder is a 4-layer MLP (hidden dimension 256) with skip connections, taking pooled suffix features (1024-d) and query points (3-d) as input. The end-effector extractor is a 2-layer MLP (1024 $\\to$ 256 $\\to$ 3).\n\n\\paragraph{Training schedule.}\nTraining proceeds in two phases:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 1} (steps 0--5000): Backbone and LoRA parameters are frozen. Only the SDF decoder and EE extractor are trained, allowing them to learn meaningful representations before the policy adapts.\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 2} (steps 5000--30000): LoRA parameters are unfrozen with a 100-step gradient warmup ramp. All auxiliary heads continue training jointly with the policy.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\begin{tabular}{ll}\n\\toprule\nParameter & Value \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase model & Pi0.5 (OpenPi, $\\sim$3.5B parameters) \\\\\nOptimizer & AdamW \\\\\nLearning rate & $2.5 \\times 10^{-5}$ (cosine decay to $\\mathrm{lr}/10$) \\\\\nWarmup steps & 1000 \\\\\nBatch size & 32 \\\\\nTotal steps & 30000 \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{sdf}$ & \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\} \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{ee}$ & 0.01 \\\\\nSDF query points per step & 64 \\\\\nSDF clamp $\\delta$ & 0.05 m \\\\\nEikonal weight & 0.1 \\\\\nLoRA rank (vision) & 16 \\\\\nLoRA rank (action) & 32 \\\\\nPhase 1 duration & 5000 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation protocol.}\nEach episode runs for at most 300 timesteps. The policy generates a 50-step action trajectory via flow matching and executes the first 5 steps before replanning. An episode terminates early upon task completion. Collision detection uses MuJoCo's built-in contact detection: any contact between the robot and a non-target object registers as a collision. For Pi0.5 evaluations, camera images are rotated 180$^\\circ$ to match the pre-training viewpoint convention; this correction was verified to restore TSR from ${\\sim}$5\\% to ${\\sim}$52\\% while leaving CAR comparable. Oracle variants use 40 episodes per task (160 total) with a single seed; all other configurations use 50 episodes per task (200 total).\n\n\\paragraph{Data.}\nWe use the SafeLIBERO object\\_pudding suite with pre-computed SDF ground truth. SDF values are computed offline from the obstacle mesh using a dense $64^3$ voxel grid. During training, 64 query points are randomly sampled per step from this grid.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational resources.}\nAll training and evaluation experiments are conducted on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\\,GB). This invariance indicates that avoidance patterns reflect the spatial statistics of collision-free demonstrations rather than geometric supervision.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task CAR (\\%) at 30K steps. $\\lambda{=}0.005$ and $\\lambda{=}0.01$ report 3-seed means. Per-task ordering (CP highest, BBQ lowest) is preserved across all conditions.}\n\\label{tab:per_task_car}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nCondition & OJ & CP & Milk & BBQ & Agg. \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA s0 & 68 & 74 & 66 & 20 & 57.0 \\\\\nLoRA s1 & 54 & 68 & 72 & 16 & 52.5 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ (3-seed) & 54.0 & 72.7 & 71.3 & 20.0 & 54.5 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ (3-seed) & 57.3 & 72.7 & 72.0 & 23.3 & 56.3 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$ s0 & 60 & 74 & 68 & 30 & 58.0 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$ s1 & 66 & 72 & 70 & 26 & 58.5 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nFor the $\\lambda = 0.1$ condition, seed~1 records avg\\_max\\_obstacle\\_displacement of 0.0553\\,m (per-task: OJ\\,=\\,0.033, CP\\,=\\,0.055, Milk\\,=\\,0.041, BBQ\\,=\\,0.093), comparable to other fine-tuned conditions and consistent with active manipulation rather than frozen behavior.\n\n\\section{Safe Success Rate Analysis}\\label{sec:appendix_ssr}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:ssr} reports the Safe Success Rate (SSR\\,=\\,TSR\\,$\\cap$\\,CAR), the fraction of episodes achieving both task completion and collision avoidance. The SSR/TSR ratio quantifies the proportion of successful episodes that are also collision-free, controlling for task-completion rate.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{SSR and SSR/TSR ratio across conditions. The ratio increase from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (fine-tuned) indicates that CAR gains reflect learned safer execution, not reduced task exposure.}\n\\label{tab:ssr}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nCondition & TSR (\\%) & CAR (\\%) & SSR (\\%) & SSR/TSR \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0.5 baseline & 74.7 & 26.8 & 25.0\\textsuperscript{a} & 0.33 \\\\\nLoRA-only (3 seeds) & 49.7 & 54.83 & 28.3 & 0.57 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ (3 seeds) & 54.0 & 54.5 & 27.8 & 0.51 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ (3 seeds) & 53.8 & 56.33 & 30.2 & 0.56 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & 48.0 & 58.25 & 26.5 & 0.55 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize \\textsuperscript{a}Estimated; per-episode overlap unavailable for 3-seed rotation-corrected protocol. \\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}SSR from seed~1 only; seed~0 per-episode data overwritten.}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\section{Per-Task Oracle Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_pertask}\n\\label{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:oracle_pertask} shows per-task CAR for oracle refinement. On task~1 (\\texttt{chocolate\\_pudding}), where the EE is the primary collision body and the LoRA baseline achieves only 46.0\\% CAR, Oracle-adaptive improves to 55.0\\% and Oracle-feature to 47.5\\%. On the remaining tasks, all oracle variants degrade: Oracle-adaptive reduces task~0 (\\texttt{orange\\_juice}) from 96.0\\% to 82.5\\% and task~2 (\\texttt{milk}) from 98.0\\% to 77.5\\%. The SDF gradient pushes the EE away from the nearest surface, but the resulting joint-space displacement rotates intermediate arm links into obstacle volumes.\n\nThe feature-space oracle fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements/episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49), and CAR (76.9\\%) shows no advantage despite 17$\\times$ more steps. The A3 learned SDF never triggers refinement (0 steps/episode), as its outputs avoid the activation threshold.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task CAR (\\%) for oracle SDF refinement variants. EE-only refinement helps only on task~1 where the EE is the primary collision body; on other tasks it trades one collision site for another.}\n\\label{tab:oracle_pertask}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Task 0 & Task 1 & Task 2 & Task 3 & Agg. \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA baseline & 96.0 & 46.0 & 98.0 & 88.0 & 82.0 \\\\\n\\midrule\nOracle-adaptive & 82.5 & 55.0 & 77.5 & 85.0 & 75.0 \\\\\nOracle-fixed & 85.0 & 50.0 & 77.5 & 80.0 & 73.1 \\\\\nOracle-feature & 90.0 & 47.5 & 82.5 & 87.5 & 76.9 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\section{Null Distribution for SDF Loss Weight Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_null}\n\nWe construct a null distribution by pooling the three LoRA-only seeds and three $\\lambda = 0.005$ seeds into six per-seed CAR measurements: 57.0\\% (LoRA s0, re-evaluated), 52.5\\% (LoRA s1), 55.0\\% (LoRA s2), 57.0\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s0), 54.0\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s1), 52.5\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s2), yielding mean 54.67\\% and standard deviation 2.04\\% (Bessel-corrected, $n{-}1 = 5$). We treat these as exchangeable because SDF at $\\lambda = 0.005$ produces no separation from LoRA-only. The $\\lambda = 0.01$ mean of 56.33\\% falls within $0.8\\sigma$ of this null ($p > 0.1$), consistent with seed-to-seed variation rather than a treatment effect. The $\\lambda = 0.1$ 30K mean of 58.25\\% (2 seeds) yields $z = 1.76$ ($p \\approx 0.039$, approximate $z$-test), a marginal departure whose interpretation is limited by $n = 2$.\n\nA Phase~2 ablation reinforces this conclusion. Setting $\\lambda = 0$ during Phase~2 (steps 5K--30K), so that SDF supervision is active only during auxiliary head warmup (Phase~1, steps 0--5K), yields CAR of 57.5\\% (single seed, s2). This value lies within the null range, indicating that SDF training produces no lasting behavioral effect on collision avoidance.\n\n\\section{Step-wise Checkpoint Analysis for \\texorpdfstring{$\\lambda = 0.1$}{lambda = 0.1}}\n\\label{sec:appendix_stepwise}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:stepwise_lambda01} reports per-seed CAR at seven checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$. Seed-1 data is available at 16K, 20K, 22K, and 30K; 24K--28K report seed-0 only. The seed-0 curve shows no monotonic trend through 28K; the 30K checkpoint marks the first consistent dual-seed departure from the null band, though whether this reflects a genuine late-training effect or sampling variance at $n = 2$ remains open.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed CAR (\\%) at training checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$ (200 episodes each). 24K--28K: seed-0 only. Dual-seed means remain within the null band (54.67\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,1.94\\%) at all checkpoints except 30K, where both seeds depart marginally ($z = 1.85$, $p \\approx 0.032$).}\n\\label{tab:stepwise_lambda01}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\nCheckpoint & Seed 0 & Seed 1 & Mean \\\\\n\\midrule\n16K steps & 55.5 & 56.0 & 55.75 \\\\\n20K steps & 55.0 & 56.0 & 55.5 \\\\\n22K steps & 60.5 & 56.5 & 58.5 \\\\\n24K steps & 52.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n26K steps & 53.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n28K steps & 54.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n30K steps & 58.0 & 58.5 & 58.25 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nIndividual seeds exhibit checkpoint-to-checkpoint CAR fluctuations of up to 8\\,pp (seed~0: 52.5--60.5\\%), larger than the cross-seed standard deviation at any fixed checkpoint ($\\leq$2.8\\,pp). This is consistent with binomial sampling noise at $n = 50$ episodes per task ($\\sigma \\approx 7$\\,pp per task for $p \\approx 0.5$). The seed-averaged curve shows no monotonic trend, confirming that SDF loss weight does not produce a progressive training signal.\n\n\n\\section{Oracle SDF Refinement Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_oracle}\n\nAll oracle variants replace the learned SDF decoder with ground-truth distance fields computed from the simulation geometry. Each is evaluated for 160 episodes (4 tasks $\\times$ 40).\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-adaptive.} At each timestep, if the ground-truth SDF at the end-effector position falls below a safety margin, the action is adjusted by a step in the negative SDF gradient direction. The step size $\\alpha$ is scaled inversely with distance: smaller distances produce larger corrections. This mimics an adaptive potential field controller.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-fixed.} Identical to Oracle-adaptive except the step size is fixed at $\\alpha = 0.005$ regardless of distance. This isolates the effect of step-size adaptation from the refinement geometry.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-feature.} Instead of modifying the action directly in Cartesian space, this variant applies the SDF gradient in the policy's latent feature space via backpropagation. The gradient of the ground-truth SDF value with respect to the intermediate features is computed and used to perturb the features before the action head decodes them. This tests whether geometric corrections are more effective when applied in the model's learned representation space. In practice, the feature-space variant fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements per episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49 in action space).\n\n\n\\section{Training Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_training}\n\n\\paragraph{Architecture.}\nWe use LoRA~\\citep{hu2022lora} adapters injected into the PaliGemma vision encoder (rank 16, $\\alpha$=16) and the action expert (rank 32, $\\alpha$=32), following the configuration of OpenPi. The SDF decoder is a 4-layer MLP (hidden dimension 256) with skip connections, taking pooled suffix features (1024-d) and query points (3-d) as input. The end-effector extractor is a 2-layer MLP (1024 $\\to$ 256 $\\to$ 3).\n\n\\paragraph{Training schedule.}\nTraining proceeds in two phases:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 1} (steps 0--5000): Backbone and LoRA parameters are frozen. Only the SDF decoder and EE extractor are trained, allowing them to learn meaningful representations before the policy adapts.\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 2} (steps 5000--30000): LoRA parameters are unfrozen with a 100-step gradient warmup ramp. All auxiliary heads continue training jointly with the policy.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\begin{tabular}{ll}\n\\toprule\nParameter & Value \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase model & Pi0.5 (OpenPi, $\\sim$3.5B parameters) \\\\\nOptimizer & AdamW \\\\\nLearning rate & $2.5 \\times 10^{-5}$ (cosine decay to $\\mathrm{lr}/10$) \\\\\nWarmup steps & 1000 \\\\\nBatch size & 32 \\\\\nTotal steps & 30000 \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{sdf}$ & \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\} \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{ee}$ & 0.01 \\\\\nSDF query points per step & 64 \\\\\nSDF clamp $\\delta$ & 0.05 m \\\\\nEikonal weight & 0.1 \\\\\nLoRA rank (vision) & 16 \\\\\nLoRA rank (action) & 32 \\\\\nPhase 1 duration & 5000 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation protocol.}\nEach episode runs for at most 300 timesteps. The policy generates a 50-step action trajectory via flow matching and executes the first 5 steps before replanning. An episode terminates early upon task completion. Collision detection uses MuJoCo's built-in contact detection: any contact between the robot and a non-target object registers as a collision. For Pi0.5 evaluations, camera images are rotated 180$^\\circ$ to match the pre-training viewpoint convention; this correction was verified to restore TSR from ${\\sim}$5\\% to ${\\sim}$52\\% while leaving CAR comparable. Oracle variants use 40 episodes per task (160 total) with a single seed; all other configurations use 50 episodes per task (200 total).\n\n\\paragraph{Data.}\nWe use the SafeLIBERO object\\_pudding suite with pre-computed SDF ground truth. SDF values are computed offline from the obstacle mesh using a dense $64^3$ voxel grid. During training, 64 query points are randomly sampled per step from this grid.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational resources.}\nAll training and evaluation experiments are conducted on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\\,GB). Phase~1 (5K steps) takes approximately 6 hours on a single GPU; Phase~2 (25K steps) takes approximately 32 hours. Evaluation of 200 episodes requires approximately 4 hours. Across 100+ experiments (training runs, evaluations, oracle variants, and ablations), the total computational budget is approximately 700 GPU-hours.\n",

Pooling the six null values $\\{57, 52.5, 55, 57, 54, 52.5\\}$ and two $\\lambda = 0.1$ values $\\{58, 58.5\\}$ into eight measurements, we enumerate all $\\binom{8}{2} = 28$ two-element combinations and compute the fraction whose mean $\\geq 58.25$. The SSR/TSR ratio quantifies the proportion of successful episodes that are also collision-free, controlling for task-completion rate.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{SSR and SSR/TSR ratio across conditions. The ratio increase from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (fine-tuned) indicates that CAR gains reflect learned safer execution, not reduced task exposure.}\n\\label{tab:ssr}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nCondition & TSR (\\%) & CAR (\\%) & SSR (\\%) & SSR/TSR \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0.5 baseline & 74.7 & 26.8 & 25.0\\textsuperscript{a} & 0.33 \\\\\nLoRA-only (3 seeds) & 49.7 & 54.83 & 28.3 & 0.57 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ (3 seeds) & 54.0 & 54.5 & 27.8 & 0.51 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ (3 seeds) & 53.8 & 56.33 & 30.2 & 0.56 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & 48.0 & 58.25 & 26.5 & 0.55 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize \\textsuperscript{a}Estimated; per-episode overlap unavailable for 3-seed rotation-corrected protocol. \\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}SSR from seed~1 only; seed~0 per-episode data overwritten.}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\section{Per-Task Oracle Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_pertask}\n\\label{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:oracle_pertask} shows per-task CAR for oracle refinement. On task~1 (\\texttt{chocolate\\_pudding}), where the EE is the primary collision body and the LoRA baseline achieves only 46.0\\% CAR, Oracle-adaptive improves to 55.0\\% and Oracle-feature to 47.5\\%. On the remaining tasks, all oracle variants degrade: Oracle-adaptive reduces task~0 (\\texttt{orange\\_juice}) from 96.0\\% to 82.5\\% and task~2 (\\texttt{milk}) from 98.0\\% to 77.5\\%. The SDF gradient pushes the EE away from the nearest surface, but the resulting joint-space displacement rotates intermediate arm links into obstacle volumes.\n\nThe feature-space oracle fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements/episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49), and CAR (76.9\\%) shows no advantage despite 17$\\times$ more steps. The A3 learned SDF never triggers refinement (0 steps/episode), as its outputs avoid the activation threshold.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task CAR (\\%) for oracle SDF refinement variants. EE-only refinement helps only on task~1 where the EE is the primary collision body; on other tasks it trades one collision site for another.}\n\\label{tab:oracle_pertask}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Task 0 & Task 1 & Task 2 & Task 3 & Agg. \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA baseline & 96.0 & 46.0 & 98.0 & 88.0 & 82.0 \\\\\n\\midrule\nOracle-adaptive & 82.5 & 55.0 & 77.5 & 85.0 & 75.0 \\\\\nOracle-fixed & 85.0 & 50.0 & 77.5 & 80.0 & 73.1 \\\\\nOracle-feature & 90.0 & 47.5 & 82.5 & 87.5 & 76.9 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\section{Null Distribution for SDF Loss Weight Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_null}\n\nWe construct a null distribution by pooling the three LoRA-only seeds and three $\\lambda = 0.005$ seeds into six per-seed CAR measurements: 57.0\\% (LoRA s0, re-evaluated), 52.5\\% (LoRA s1), 55.0\\% (LoRA s2), 57.0\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s0), 54.0\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s1), 52.5\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s2), yielding mean 54.67\\% and standard deviation 2.04\\% (Bessel-corrected, $n{-}1 = 5$). We treat these as exchangeable because SDF at $\\lambda = 0.005$ produces no separation from LoRA-only. The $\\lambda = 0.01$ mean of 56.33\\% falls within $0.8\\sigma$ of this null ($p > 0.1$), consistent with seed-to-seed variation rather than a treatment effect. The $\\lambda = 0.1$ 30K mean of 58.25\\% (2 seeds) yields $z = 1.76$ ($p \\approx 0.039$, approximate $z$-test), a marginal departure whose interpretation is limited by $n = 2$.\n\nA Phase~2 ablation reinforces this conclusion. Setting $\\lambda = 0$ during Phase~2 (steps 5K--30K), so that SDF supervision is active only during auxiliary head warmup (Phase~1, steps 0--5K), yields CAR of 57.5\\% (single seed, s2). This value lies within the null range, indicating that SDF training produces no lasting behavioral effect on collision avoidance.\n\n\\section{Step-wise Checkpoint Analysis for \\texorpdfstring{$\\lambda = 0.1$}{lambda = 0.1}}\n\\label{sec:appendix_stepwise}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:stepwise_lambda01} reports per-seed CAR at seven checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$. Seed-1 data is available at 16K, 20K, 22K, and 30K; 24K--28K report seed-0 only. The seed-0 curve shows no monotonic trend through 28K; the 30K checkpoint marks the first consistent dual-seed departure from the null band, though whether this reflects a genuine late-training effect or sampling variance at $n = 2$ remains open.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed CAR (\\%) at training checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$ (200 episodes each). 24K--28K: seed-0 only. Dual-seed means remain within the null band (54.67\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,2.04\\%) at all checkpoints except 30K, where both seeds depart marginally ($z = 1.76$, $p \\approx 0.039$).}\n\\label{tab:stepwise_lambda01}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\nCheckpoint & Seed 0 & Seed 1 & Mean \\\\\n\\midrule\n16K steps & 55.5 & 56.0 & 55.75 \\\\\n20K steps & 55.0 & 56.0 & 55.5 \\\\\n22K steps & 60.5 & 56.5 & 58.5 \\\\\n24K steps & 52.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n26K steps & 53.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n28K steps & 54.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n30K steps & 58.0 & 58.5 & 58.25 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nIndividual seeds exhibit checkpoint-to-checkpoint CAR fluctuations of up to 8\\,pp (seed~0: 52.5--60.5\\%), larger than the cross-seed standard deviation at any fixed checkpoint ($\\leq$2.8\\,pp). This is consistent with binomial sampling noise at $n = 50$ episodes per task ($\\sigma \\approx 7$\\,pp per task for $p \\approx 0.5$). The seed-averaged curve shows no monotonic trend, confirming that SDF loss weight does not produce a progressive training signal.\n\n\n\\section{Oracle SDF Refinement Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_oracle}\n\nAll oracle variants replace the learned SDF decoder with ground-truth distance fields computed from the simulation geometry. Each is evaluated for 160 episodes (4 tasks $\\times$ 40).\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-adaptive.} At each timestep, if the ground-truth SDF at the end-effector position falls below a safety margin, the action is adjusted by a step in the negative SDF gradient direction. The step size $\\alpha$ is scaled inversely with distance: smaller distances produce larger corrections. This mimics an adaptive potential field controller.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-fixed.} Identical to Oracle-adaptive except the step size is fixed at $\\alpha = 0.005$ regardless of distance. This isolates the effect of step-size adaptation from the refinement geometry.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-feature.} Instead of modifying the action directly in Cartesian space, this variant applies the SDF gradient in the policy's latent feature space via backpropagation. The gradient of the ground-truth SDF value with respect to the intermediate features is computed and used to perturb the features before the action head decodes them. This tests whether geometric corrections are more effective when applied in the model's learned representation space. In practice, the feature-space variant fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements per episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49 in action space).\n\n\n\\section{Training Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_training}\n\n\\paragraph{Architecture.}\nWe use LoRA~\\citep{hu2022lora} adapters injected into the PaliGemma vision encoder (rank 16, $\\alpha$=16) and the action expert (rank 32, $\\alpha$=32), following the configuration of OpenPi. The SDF decoder is a 4-layer MLP (hidden dimension 256) with skip connections, taking pooled suffix features (1024-d) and query points (3-d) as input. The end-effector extractor is a 2-layer MLP (1024 $\\to$ 256 $\\to$ 3).\n\n\\paragraph{Training schedule.}\nTraining proceeds in two phases:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 1} (steps 0--5000): Backbone and LoRA parameters are frozen. Only the SDF decoder and EE extractor are trained, allowing them to learn meaningful representations before the policy adapts.\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 2} (steps 5000--30000): LoRA parameters are unfrozen with a 100-step gradient warmup ramp. All auxiliary heads continue training jointly with the policy.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\begin{tabular}{ll}\n\\toprule\nParameter & Value \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase model & Pi0.5 (OpenPi, $\\sim$3.5B parameters) \\\\\nOptimizer & AdamW \\\\\nLearning rate & $2.5 \\times 10^{-5}$ (cosine decay to $\\mathrm{lr}/10$) \\\\\nWarmup steps & 1000 \\\\\nBatch size & 32 \\\\\nTotal steps & 30000 \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{sdf}$ & \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\} \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{ee}$ & 0.01 \\\\\nSDF query points per step & 64 \\\\\nSDF clamp $\\delta$ & 0.05 m \\\\\nEikonal weight & 0.1 \\\\\nLoRA rank (vision) & 16 \\\\\nLoRA rank (action) & 32 \\\\\nPhase 1 duration & 5000 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation protocol.}\nEach episode runs for at most 300 timesteps. The policy generates a 50-step action trajectory via flow matching and executes the first 5 steps before replanning. An episode terminates early upon task completion. Collision detection uses MuJoCo's built-in contact detection: any contact between the robot and a non-target object registers as a collision. For Pi0.5 evaluations, camera images are rotated 180$^\\circ$ to match the pre-training viewpoint convention; this correction was verified to restore TSR from ${\\sim}$5\\% to ${\\sim}$52\\% while leaving CAR comparable. Oracle variants use 40 episodes per task (160 total) with a single seed; all other configurations use 50 episodes per task (200 total).\n\n\\paragraph{Data.}\nWe use the SafeLIBERO object\\_pudding suite with pre-computed SDF ground truth. SDF values are computed offline from the obstacle mesh using a dense $64^3$ voxel grid. During training, 64 query points are randomly sampled per step from this grid.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational resources.}\nAll training and evaluation experiments are conducted on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\\,GB). Phase~1 (5K steps) takes approximately 6 hours on a single GPU; Phase~2 (25K steps) takes approximately 32 hours. Evaluation of 200 episodes requires approximately 4 hours. Across 100+ experiments (training runs, evaluations, oracle variants, and ablations), the total computational budget is approximately 700 GPU-hours.\n",

## Permutation test 结果

- 8 个值: {57, 52.5, 55, 57, 54, 52.5, 58, 58.5}
- C(8,2) = 28 种组合
- 均值 ≥ 58.25 的组合: 仅 {58, 58.5}（均值 = 58.25），共 1 种
- **exact permutation p = 1/28 ≈ 0.036**
- 与 approximate z-test p ≈ 0.039 一致
